Bootstrap-only files per cardinal-rule-5 §"Bootstrap-only files (the ONLY hand-coded artifacts permitted in this runtime)" are STILL subject to cardinal-rule-8 — their content must adapt SOTA patterns (env-block from CCBP, launcher entrypoint from `code.claude.com/docs/en/setup`, cardinal-rule numbering from sibling claude-sota, install-discipline patterns from upstream tool docs). Cardinal-rule-8 is the destination of "zero self inventing" — strictly enforced via Wave 50 fire 3+ adversarial-review gate.
84:   **Conformance verification mechanism (per Wave 50 Agent C-redo P2-3)**: per-row `CR-8 status` column in manifest Section 0 (and recommended for Sections 11/11.5/14/14.5 cite-class rows) carrying values `ADAPTED-FROM-SOTA` (cite-anchor present + verified) / `NOVEL-DOCUMENTED-EXCEPTION` (bootstrap necessity + rationale) / `PENDING-AUDIT` (default; awaits adversarial-review gate). Cardinal-rule-8 is checkable, not just declared.
88:## NEW Cardinal Rule for this runtime (cardinal-rule-9 install-risk discipline — Wave 50 fire 4 from Agent F archaeology)
90:9. **Install-risk discipline — version pin all `@latest`, expect 2-round fix-forward per hook install, pre-cite-import REVERT check.** Per Agent F sibling-archaeology evidence (90-100% bug-magnet ratios on settings.json/sss.ps1/codex hooks/.mcp.json) — install discipline mandates:
91:   - **Version-pin mandate**: every `@latest` install command in manifest install rows MUST carry version pin OR explicit `@latest-acknowledged-D6-risk` marker. Sibling `.mcp.json` 22/23 commits are D6 today-release-auto-upgrade fires — `@latest` without acknowledgment violates cardinal-rule-9.
93:   - **Pre-cite-import REVERT check**: BEFORE any sibling cite-import per Section 14/14.5 exception, run `git -C Z:/claude-sota log --all --oneline -- '<sibling-target-path>'` to detect REVERT-AND-REMOVE precedents. If found (3 known: `bash_command_allowlist.py` / `fleet_health_start.py` / `permission_request_auto_approve.py`), do NOT re-install per `Z:/claude-sota/.claude/projects/Z--claude-sota/memory/feedback_check_gitignore_before_porting.md` "harness has decided" pattern.
94:   - **Sibling-bleed defense**: every **install-class** cite-import containing `Z:/claude-sota/` paths or sibling-specific env values (`CLAUDE_CODE_PROJECT_DIR='Z:/claude-sota-state/...'`, `gitnexus --repo claude-sota`, `additionalDirectory` entries) MUST be path-rewritten for claude-sota-installed context BEFORE install. Sibling-bleed is the dominant post-bootstrap failure class per Agent F first-50-commits archaeology (4 cleanup commits in first 42).
95:   - **Read-only research probe exception** (per Wave 50 fire 8 Pattern A on Agent I F-5): the sibling-bleed rule above scopes to **install-class** cite-imports only. **Read-only research probes** by sota-researcher subagent (per manifest §Section 18 research workflow) and **immutable cite-anchors** at `Z:/repos/deps/<repo>/file:line @ HEAD <SHA>` are EXEMPT — these consume sibling/upstream state as RESEARCH input, not as RUNTIME dependency, and produce no install-class artifact. Exemption scope (concrete enumeration):
96:     - (i) `Z:/repos/deps/<repo>/file:line @ HEAD <SHA>` cite-anchors (immutable at SHA — TIER-1-DIRECT upstream sources)
97:     - (ii) `Z:/claude-sota/.claude/projects/Z--claude-sota/memory/*.md` decision-history files read by sota-researcher for Mia probes per manifest §18.2 (NOT installed; read-class only per cardinal-rule-10 step 2 INVOKE workflow)

100:     INSTALL-class cite-imports per Section 14/14.5 STILL require path-rewrite per the sibling-bleed defense above. Distinction: read-only research probe surfaces sibling state as RESEARCH evidence into orchestrator-side reasoning; install-class cite-import COPIES sibling content into runtime where any unrewritten path becomes a runtime dependency.
104:## NEW Cardinal Rule for this runtime (cardinal-rule-10 research-first-then-install)
106:10. **Research-first-then-install — every build/error/unknown encountered in this runtime triggers SOTA research BEFORE remediation.** When claude-sota-installed hits a build failure / dependency error / unknown configuration / install ambiguity / new feature requirement, the FIRST action is SOTA research (find canonical repo / pattern / cite anchor that solves the class) BEFORE attempting fix. Remediation paths in priority order:
107:    - **(a) Install canonical SOTA solution** — research surfaces an upstream repo that solves the class (e.g., FM-17.d watchdog stall → install `fm17d_stall_detector.py` from sibling cite-import per Section 13 — corrected from prior stale "Section 13.1" reference per Wave 50 fire 10 Pattern A on Agent I F-14 + Agent L Dimension 3 P3)
108:    - **(b) Cite-adapt SOTA pattern** — research surfaces a SOTA pattern that doesn't have a single install-class implementation; adapt the pattern with file:line cite anchor per cardinal-rule-8 (e.g., superpowers `subagent-driven-development` 2-stage review pattern)
112:    **Implication for runtime architecture**: sota-researcher subagent MUST install in **Tier 1** (not Tier 5 per Agent E install order) — it's the first non-foundational install because it drives every subsequent install decision. sota-researcher is the load-bearing primitive enabling cardinal-rule-10 conformance. See manifest §Section 18 — Research Architecture for full design.
114:    **Build-error remediation specifics**: when codex T1 / T2 / T3 returns NEEDS-REVISION with prescribed-edits referring to upstream APIs, libraries, patterns the runtime doesn't have installed yet — DO NOT hand-code the fix. Research the upstream first (gh search, mcp__github__search_repositories, mcp__exa__web_search_exa, mcp__deepwiki__ask_question, mcp__perplexity__perplexity_search), identify the SOTA install-or-cite source, then install per Section 18 research-then-install workflow. Per cardinal-rule-5 + 6 + 8: hand-coding a fix that an upstream SOTA repo provides is a quadruple violation.

Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\git-cli-grammar-discipline.md:18:- **Trigger incident** (n=1): 2026-05-03 commit attempt on Ship 2 used `git commit --only -- .claude/settings.json -F tmp/ship2-commit-msg.txt` → git returned `error: pathspec '-F' did not match` (correct — `-F` after `--` becomes pathspec). Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\git-cli-grammar-discipline.md:26:2. **`--` ends option processing; subsequent tokens take the command's post-separator grammar.** Verbatim from git-commit OPTIONS section: "Do not interpret any more arguments as options." For commands whose post-separator grammar is `<pathspec>...` or `[<pathspec>...]` (commit, add, checkout path forms, restore, diff, log, stash push, bisect start), later `-F`, `-m`, `-S`, `--only`, `--no-verify`, etc. are pathspec strings, not flags — they fail to match because no pathspec by that name exists. For revision/path-disambiguating commands (rev-parse, show, log/diff with revision args, checkout branch forms), `--` separates revisions from paths; use `--end-of-options` (invariant #4) when the thing to protect is a revision/object argument. Cross-impl evidence: libgit2 `git_pathspec_new()` (pathspec.h L84-L90), gitoxide `Pattern` parser (gix-pathspec/src/lib.rs L177-L178) — both implementations parse post-separator tokens as typed Pattern objects, never as flag candidates.
Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\git-cli-grammar-discipline.md:37:2. Specifically: `git commit -F <file> -- <pathspec>`, `git diff <flags> -- <paths>`, `git log -1 HEAD -- <paths>`, `git checkout <flags> -- <paths>`, `git restore <flags> -- <paths>`

 succeeded in 355ms:
   1: # Rust/codex-rs
   2: 
   3: In the codex-rs folder where the rust code lives:
   4: 
   5: - Crate names are prefixed with `codex-`. For example, the `core` folder's crate is named `codex-core`
   6: - When using format! and you can inline variables into {}, always do that.
   7: - Install any commands the repo relies on (for example `just`, `rg`, or `cargo-insta`) if they aren't already available before running instructions here.
   8: - Never add or modify any code related to `CODEX_SANDBOX_NETWORK_DISABLED_ENV_VAR` or `CODEX_SANDBOX_ENV_VAR`.
   9:   - You operate in a sandbox where `CODEX_SANDBOX_NETWORK_DISABLED=1` will be set whenever you use the `shell` tool. Any existing code that uses `CODEX_SANDBOX_NETWORK_DISABLED_ENV_VAR` was authored with this fact in mind. It is often used to early exit out of tests that the author knew you would not be able to run given your sandbox limitations.
  10:   - Similarly, when you spawn a process using Seatbelt (`/usr/bin/sandbox-exec`), `CODEX_SANDBOX=seatbelt` will be set on the child process. Integration tests that want to run Seatbelt themselves cannot be run under Seatbelt, so checks for `CODEX_SANDBOX=seatbelt` are also often used to early exit out of tests, as appropriate.
  11: - Always collapse if statements per https://rust-lang.github.io/rust-clippy/master/index.html#collapsible_if
  12: - Always inline format! args when possible per https://rust-lang.github.io/rust-clippy/master/index.html#uninlined_format_args
  13: - Use method references over closures when possible per https://rust-lang.github.io/rust-clippy/master/index.html#redundant_closure_for_method_calls
  14: - Avoid bool or ambiguous `Option` parameters that force callers to write hard-to-read code such as `foo(false)` or `bar(None)`. Prefer enums, named methods, newtypes, or other idiomatic Rust API shapes when they keep the callsite self-documenting.
  15: - When you cannot make that API change and still need a small positional-literal callsite in Rust, follow the `argument_comment_lint` convention:
  16:   - Use an exact `/*param_name*/` comment before opaque literal arguments such as `None`, booleans, and numeric literals when passing them by position.
  17:   - Do not add these comments for string or char literals unless the comment adds real clarity; those literals are intentionally exempt from the lint.
  18:   - The parameter name in the comment must exactly match the callee signature.
  19:   - You can run `just argument-comment-lint` to run the lint check locally. This is powered by Bazel, so running it the first time can be slow if Bazel is not warmed up, though incremental invocations should take <15s. Most of the time, it is best to update the PR and let CI take responsibility for checking this (or run it asynchronously in the background after submitting the PR). Note CI checks all three platforms, which the local run does not.
  20: - When possible, make `match` statements exhaustive and avoid wildcard arms.
  21: - Newly added traits should include doc comments that explain their role and how implementations are expected to use them.
  22: - When writing tests, prefer comparing the equality of entire objects over fields one by one.
  23: - When making a change that adds or changes an API, ensure that the documentation in the `docs/` folder is up to date if applicable.
  24: - Prefer private modules and explicitly exported public crate API.
  25: - If you change `ConfigToml` or nested config types, run `just write-config-schema` to update `codex-rs/core/config.schema.json`.
  26: - When working with MCP tool calls, prefer using `codex-rs/codex-mcp/src/mcp_connection_manager.rs` to handle mutation of tools and tool calls. Aim to minimize the footprint of changes and leverage existing abstractions rather than plumbing code through multiple levels of function calls.
  27: - If you change Rust dependencies (`Cargo.toml` or `Cargo.lock`), run `just bazel-lock-update` from the
  28:   repo root to refresh `MODULE.bazel.lock`, and include that lockfile update in the same change.
  29: - After dependency changes, run `just bazel-lock-check` from the repo root so lockfile drift is caught
  30:   locally before CI.
  31: - Bazel does not automatically make source-tree files available to compile-time Rust file access. If
  32:   you add `include_str!`, `include_bytes!`, `sqlx::migrate!`, or similar build-time file or
  33:   directory reads, update the crate's `BUILD.bazel` (`compile_data`, `build_script_data`, or test
  34:   data) or Bazel may fail even when Cargo passes.
  35: - Do not create small helper methods that are referenced only once.
  36: - Avoid large modules:
  37:   - Prefer adding new modules instead of growing existing ones.
  38:   - Target Rust modules under 500 LoC, excluding tests.
  39:   - If a file exceeds roughly 800 LoC, add new functionality in a new module instead of extending
  40:     the existing file unless there is a strong documented reason not to.
  41:   - This rule applies especially to high-touch files that already attract unrelated changes, such
  42:     as `codex-rs/tui/src/app.rs`, `codex-rs/tui/src/bottom_pane/chat_composer.rs`,
  43:     `codex-rs/tui/src/bottom_pane/footer.rs`, `codex-rs/tui/src/chatwidget.rs`,
  44:     `codex-rs/tui/src/bottom_pane/mod.rs`, and similarly central orchestration modules.
  45:   - When extracting code from a large module, move the related tests and module/type docs toward
  46:     the new implementation so the invariants stay close to the code that owns them.
  47:   - Avoid adding new standalone methods to `codex-rs/tui/src/chatwidget.rs` unless the change is
  48:     trivial; prefer new modules/files and keep `chatwidget.rs` focused on orchestration.
  49: - When running Rust commands (e.g. `just fix` or `cargo test`) be patient with the command and never try to kill them using the PID. Rust lock can make the execution slow, this is expected.
  50: 
  51: Run `just fmt` (in `codex-rs` directory) automatically after you have finished making Rust code changes; do not ask for approval to run it. Additionally, run the tests:
  52: 
  53: 1. Run the test for the specific project that was changed. For example, if changes were made in `codex-rs/tui`, run `cargo test -p codex-tui`.
  54: 2. Once those pass, if any changes were made in common, core, or protocol, run the complete test suite with `cargo test` (or `just test` if `cargo-nextest` is installed). Avoid `--all-features` for routine local runs because it expands the build matrix and can significantly increase `target/` disk usage; use it only when you specifically need full feature coverage. project-specific or individual tests can be run without asking the user, but do ask the user before running the complete test suite.
  55: 
  56: Before finalizing a large change to `codex-rs`, run `just fix -p <project>` (in `codex-rs` directory) to fix any linter issues in the code. Prefer scoping with `-p` to avoid slow workspace‑wide Clippy builds; only run `just fix` without `-p` if you changed shared crates. Do not re-run tests after running `fix` or `fmt`.
  57: 
  58: ## The `codex-core` crate
  59: 
  60: Over time, the `codex-core` crate (defined in `codex-rs/core/`) has become bloated because it is the largest crate, so it is often easier to add something new to `codex-core` rather than refactor out the library code you need so your new code neither takes a dependency on, nor contributes to the size of, `codex-core`.
  61: 
  62: To that end: **resist adding code to codex-core**!
  63: 
  64: Particularly when introducing a new concept/feature/API, before adding to `codex-core`, consider whether:
  65: 
  66: - There is an existing crate other than `codex-core` that is an appropriate place for your new code to live.
  67: - It is time to introduce a new crate to the Cargo workspace for your new functionality. Refactor existing code as necessary to make this happen.
  68: 
  69: Likewise, when reviewing code, do not hesitate to push back on PRs that would unnecessarily add code to `codex-core`.
  70: 
  71: ## TUI style conventions
  72: 
  73: See `codex-rs/tui/styles.md`.
  74: 
  75: ## TUI code conventions
  76: 
  77: - Use concise styling helpers from ratatui’s Stylize trait.
  78:   - Basic spans: use "text".into()
  79:   - Styled spans: use "text".red(), "text".green(), "text".magenta(), "text".dim(), etc.
  80:   - Prefer these over constructing styles with `Span::styled` and `Style` directly.
  81:   - Example: patch summary file lines
  82:     - Desired: vec!["  └ ".into(), "M".red(), " ".dim(), "tui/src/app.rs".dim()]
  83: 
  84: ### TUI Styling (ratatui)
  85: 
  86: - Prefer Stylize helpers: use "text".dim(), .bold(), .cyan(), .italic(), .underlined() instead of manual Style where possible.
  87: - Prefer simple conversions: use "text".into() for spans and vec![…].into() for lines; when inference is ambiguous (e.g., Paragraph::new/Cell::from), use Line::from(spans) or Span::from(text).
  88: - Computed styles: if the Style is computed at runtime, using `Span::styled` is OK (`Span::from(text).set_style(style)` is also acceptable).
  89: - Avoid hardcoded white: do not use `.white()`; prefer the default foreground (no color).
  90: - Chaining: combine helpers by chaining for readability (e.g., url.cyan().underlined()).
  91: - Single items: prefer "text".into(); use Line::from(text) or Span::from(text) only when the target type isn’t obvious from context, or when using .into() would require extra type annotations.
  92: - Building lines: use vec![…].into() to construct a Line when the target type is obvious and no extra type annotations are needed; otherwise use Line::from(vec![…]).
  93: - Avoid churn: don’t refactor between equivalent forms (Span::styled ↔ set_style, Line::from ↔ .into()) without a clear readability or functional gain; follow file‑local conventions and do not introduce type annotations solely to satisfy .into().
  94: - Compactness: prefer the form that stays on one line after rustfmt; if only one of Line::from(vec![…]) or vec![…].into() avoids wrapping, choose that. If both wrap, pick the one with fewer wrapped lines.
  95: 
  96: ### Text wrapping
  97: 
  98: - Always use textwrap::wrap to wrap plain strings.
  99: - If you have a ratatui Line and you want to wrap it, use the helpers in tui/src/wrapping.rs, e.g. word_wrap_lines / word_wrap_line.
 100: - If you need to indent wrapped lines, use the initial_indent / subsequent_indent options from RtOptions if you can, rather than writing custom logic.
 101: - If you have a list of lines and you need to prefix them all with some prefix (optionally different on the first vs subsequent lines), use the `prefix_lines` helper from line_utils.
 102: 
 103: ## Tests
 104: 
 105: ### Snapshot tests
 106: 
 107: This repo uses snapshot tests (via `insta`), especially in `codex-rs/tui`, to validate rendered output.
 108: 
 109: **Requirement:** any change that affects user-visible UI (including adding new UI) must include
 110: corresponding `insta` snapshot coverage (add a new snapshot test if one doesn't exist yet, or
 111: update the existing snapshot). Review and accept snapshot updates as part of the PR so UI impact
 112: is easy to review and future diffs stay visual.
 113: 
 114: When UI or text output changes intentionally, update the snapshots as follows:
 115: 
 116: - Run tests to generate any updated snapshots:
 117:   - `cargo test -p codex-tui`
 118: - Check what’s pending:
 119:   - `cargo insta pending-snapshots -p codex-tui`
 120: - Review changes by reading the generated `*.snap.new` files directly in the repo, or preview a specific file:
 121:   - `cargo insta show -p codex-tui path/to/file.snap.new`
 122: - Only if you intend to accept all new snapshots in this crate, run:
 123:   - `cargo insta accept -p codex-tui`
 124: 
 125: If you don’t have the tool:
 126: 
 127: - `cargo install cargo-insta`
 128: 
 129: ### Test assertions
 130: 
 131: - Tests should use pretty_assertions::assert_eq for clearer diffs. Import this at the top of the test module if it isn't already.
 132: - Prefer deep equals comparisons whenever possible. Perform `assert_eq!()` on entire objects, rather than individual fields.
 133: - Avoid mutating process environment in tests; prefer passing environment-derived flags or dependencies from above.
 134: 
 135: ### Spawning workspace binaries in tests (Cargo vs Bazel)
 136: 
 137: - Prefer `codex_utils_cargo_bin::cargo_bin("...")` over `assert_cmd::Command::cargo_bin(...)` or `escargot` when tests need to spawn first-party binaries.
 138:   - Under Bazel, binaries and resources may live under runfiles; use `codex_utils_cargo_bin::cargo_bin` to resolve absolute paths that remain stable after `chdir`.
 139: - When locating fixture files or test resources under Bazel, avoid `env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR")`. Prefer `codex_utils_cargo_bin::find_resource!` so paths resolve correctly under both Cargo and Bazel runfiles.
 140: 
 141: ### Integration tests (core)
 142: 
 143: - Prefer the utilities in `core_test_support::responses` when writing end-to-end Codex tests.
 144: 
 145: - All `mount_sse*` helpers return a `ResponseMock`; hold onto it so you can assert against outbound `/responses` POST bodies.
 146: - Use `ResponseMock::single_request()` when a test should only issue one POST, or `ResponseMock::requests()` to inspect every captured `ResponsesRequest`.
 147: - `ResponsesRequest` exposes helpers (`body_json`, `input`, `function_call_output`, `custom_tool_call_output`, `call_output`, `header`, `path`, `query_param`) so assertions can target structured payloads instead of manual JSON digging.
 148: - Build SSE payloads with the provided `ev_*` constructors and the `sse(...)`.
 149: - Prefer `wait_for_event` over `wait_for_event_with_timeout`.
 150: - Prefer `mount_sse_once` over `mount_sse_once_match` or `mount_sse_sequence`
 151: 
 152: - Typical pattern:
 153: 
 154:   ```rust
 155:   let mock = responses::mount_sse_once(&server, responses::sse(vec![
 156:       responses::ev_response_created("resp-1"),
 157:       responses::ev_function_call(call_id, "shell", &serde_json::to_string(&args)?),
 158:       responses::ev_completed("resp-1"),
 159:   ])).await;
 160: 
 161:   codex.submit(Op::UserTurn { ... }).await?;
 162: 
 163:   // Assert request body if needed.
 164:   let request = mock.single_request();
 165:   // assert using request.function_call_output(call_id) or request.json_body() or other helpers.
 166:   ```
 167: 
 168: ## App-server API Development Best Practices
 169: 
 170: These guidelines apply to app-server protocol work in `codex-rs`, especially:
 171: 
 172: - `app-server-protocol/src/protocol/common.rs`
 173: - `app-server-protocol/src/protocol/v2.rs`
 174: - `app-server/README.md`
 175: 
 176: ### Core Rules
 177: 
 178: - All active API development should happen in app-server v2. Do not add new API surface area to v1.
 179: - Follow payload naming consistently:
 180:   `*Params` for request payloads, `*Response` for responses, and `*Notification` for notifications.
 181: - Expose RPC methods as `<resource>/<method>` and keep `<resource>` singular (for example, `thread/read`, `app/list`).
 182: - Always expose fields as camelCase on the wire with `#[serde(rename_all = "camelCase")]` unless a tagged union or explicit compatibility requirement needs a targeted rename.
 183: - Exception: config RPC payloads are expected to use snake_case to mirror config.toml keys (see the config read/write/list APIs in `app-server-protocol/src/protocol/v2.rs`).
 184: - Always set `#[ts(export_to = "v2/")]` on v2 request/response/notification types so generated TypeScript lands in the correct namespace.
 185: - Never use `#[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]` for v2 API payload fields.
 186:   Exception: client->server requests that intentionally have no params may use:
 187:   `params: #[ts(type = "undefined")] #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")] Option<()>`.
 188: - Keep Rust and TS wire renames aligned. If a field or variant uses `#[serde(rename = "...")]`, add matching `#[ts(rename = "...")]`.
 189: - For discriminated unions, use explicit tagging in both serializers:
 190:   `#[serde(tag = "type", ...)]` and `#[ts(tag = "type", ...)]`.
 191: - Prefer plain `String` IDs at the API boundary (do UUID parsing/conversion internally if needed).
 192: - Timestamps should be integer Unix seconds (`i64`) and named `*_at` (for example, `created_at`, `updated_at`, `resets_at`).
 193: - For experimental API surface area:
 194:   use `#[experimental("method/or/field")]`, derive `ExperimentalApi` when field-level gating is needed, and use `inspect_params: true` in `common.rs` when only some fields of a method are experimental.
 195: 
 196: ### Client->server request payloads (`*Params`)
 197: 
 198: - Every optional field must be annotated with `#[ts(optional = nullable)]`. Do not use `#[ts(optional = nullable)]` outside client->server request payloads (`*Params`).
 199: - Optional collection fields (for example `Vec`, `HashMap`) must use `Option<...>` + `#[ts(optional = nullable)]`. Do not use `#[serde(default)]` to model optional collections, and do not use `skip_serializing_if` on v2 payload fields.
 200: - When you want omission to mean `false` for boolean fields, use `#[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "std::ops::Not::not")] pub field: bool` over `Option<bool>`.
 201: - For new list methods, implement cursor pagination by default:
 202:   request fields `pub cursor: Option<String>` and `pub limit: Option<u32>`,
 203:   response fields `pub data: Vec<...>` and `pub next_cursor: Option<String>`.
 204: 
 205: ### Development Workflow
 206: 
 207: - Update docs/examples when API behavior changes (at minimum `app-server/README.md`).
 208: - Regenerate schema fixtures when API shapes change:
 209:   `just write-app-server-schema`
 210:   (and `just write-app-server-schema --experimental` when experimental API fixtures are affected).
 211: - Validate with `cargo test -p codex-app-server-protocol`.
 212: - Avoid boilerplate tests that only assert experimental field markers for individual
 213:   request fields in `common.rs`; rely on schema generation/tests and behavioral coverage instead.

 succeeded in 374ms:
 108: 4. **Atomic batch** — bundle related edits (root + MEMORY.md close entry) in a single `git add ... && git commit` invocation to minimize cross-session bundling risk. Specifically: parallel sessions can commit during the gap between `git add` and `git commit`, and bundle your staged edit into their unrelated commit. The single-invocation form prevents this race.
 109: 
 110: 5. **Verify next audit fires `any_drift: false`** for the closed axis — if not, the fix didn't take. Codex postcommit review on the closing commit IS the verification gate.
 111: 
 112: ## Why
 113: 
 114: Audit-failure is the worst defect class: a broken audit looks like it's working (entries appear in JSONL) while providing 0 protection. Worse: a working audit that surfaces drift but isn't actioned has the same effect — the mechanization runs, the data accumulates, but nothing closes the drift. Within 12-24h of accumulation, drift becomes "background noise" that humans/agents stop checking. The audit-action-loop is the consumer-side discipline that makes wired audits valuable.
 115: 
 116: ## When NOT to apply
 117: 
 118: - **Single-fire saturation** — if 4+ drifts surface in one cycle, picking ALL of them violates ONE-LOGICAL-UNIT-PER-FIRE. Pick highest-leverage; defer rest.
 119: - **Audit script itself is broken** — if JSONL records are empty (e.g. `{"ts":"..."}` only) or use wrong field names, fix the audit before closing drifts based on its output.
 120: - **Drift is intentional** — sometimes a count claim is stale because it's awaiting a related ship. Don't close pre-emptively if the related ship is imminent (next 1-2 fires).
 121: - **Cross-session ownership unclear** — if working tree shows uncommitted changes from a parallel session, prefer `git stash` + commit + restore over editing in-place; minimizes bundling risk.
 122: 
 123: ## Related
 124: 
 125: - `.claude/rules/research-protocol.md` VERIFY corollary 2 (Audit producer-consumer coupling) — claude-sota-local invariant for this rule. Research-protocol's corollary 2 says "every state-correctness audit needs BOTH a write-side trigger wire AND a read-side consumer that surfaces regressions to a decision surface". This rule (`audit-action-loop.md`) IS the consumer-side closure discipline that satisfies that invariant.
 126: - `.claude/rules/closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md` — sister rule for the iteration-loop-during-edit moment; this rule covers the post-audit drift-closure moment.

  16: 
  17: ## Frontmatter Fields (16)
  18: 
  19: | Field | Type | Required | Description |
  20: |-------|------|----------|-------------|
  21: | `name` | string | Yes | Unique identifier using lowercase letters and hyphens |
  22: | `description` | string | Yes | When to invoke. Use `"PROACTIVELY"` for auto-invocation by Claude |
  23: | `tools` | string/list | No | Comma-separated allowlist of tools (e.g., `Read, Write, Edit, Bash`). Inherits all tools if omitted. Supports `Agent(agent_type)` syntax to restrict spawnable subagents; the older `Task(agent_type)` alias still works |
  24: | `disallowedTools` | string/list | No | Tools to deny, removed from inherited or specified list |
  25: | `model` | string | No | Model to use: `sonnet`, `opus`, `haiku`, a full model ID (e.g., `claude-opus-4-6`), or `inherit` (default: `inherit`) |
  26: | `permissionMode` | string | No | Permission mode: `default`, `acceptEdits`, `auto`, `dontAsk`, `bypassPermissions`, or `plan` |
  27: | `maxTurns` | integer | No | Maximum number of agentic turns before the subagent stops |
  28: | `skills` | list | No | Skill names to preload into agent context at startup (full content injected, not just made available) |
  29: | `mcpServers` | list | No | MCP servers for this subagent — server name strings or inline `{name: config}` objects |
  30: | `hooks` | object | No | Lifecycle hooks scoped to this subagent. All hook events are supported; `PreToolUse`, `PostToolUse`, and `Stop` are the most common |
  31: | `memory` | string | No | Persistent memory scope: `user`, `project`, or `local` |
  32: | `background` | boolean | No | Set to `true` to always run as a background task (default: `false`) |
  33: | `effort` | string | No | Effort level override when this subagent is active: `low`, `medium`, `high`, `xhigh`, `max` (Opus 4.6 only). Default: inherits from session |
  34: | `isolation` | string | No | Set to `"worktree"` to run in a temporary git worktree (auto-cleaned if no changes) |
  35: | `initialPrompt` | string | No | Auto-submitted as the first user turn when this agent runs as the main session agent (via `--agent` or the `agent` setting). Commands and skills are processed. Prepended to any user-provided prompt |
  36: | `color` | string | No | Display color for the subagent in the task list and transcript: `red`, `blue`, `green`, `yellow`, `purple`, `orange`, `pink`, or `cyan` |
  37: 
  38: ---
  39: 
  40: ## ![Official](../!/tags/official.svg) **(5)**
  41: 
  42: | # | Agent | Model | Tools | Description |
  43: |---|-------|-------|-------|-------------|
  44: | 1 | `general-purpose` | inherit | All | Complex multi-step tasks — the default agent type for research, code search, and autonomous work |
  45: | 2 | `Explore` | haiku | Read-only (no Write, Edit) | Fast codebase search and exploration — optimized for finding files, searching code, and answering codebase questions |
  46: | 3 | `Plan` | inherit | Read-only (no Write, Edit) | Pre-planning research in plan mode — explores the codebase and designs implementation approaches before writing code |
  47: | 4 | `statusline-setup` | sonnet | Read, Edit | Configures the user's Claude Code status line setting |
  48: | 5 | `claude-code-guide` | haiku | Glob, Grep, Read, WebFetch, WebSearch | Answers questions about Claude Code features, Agent SDK, and Claude API |

35:| `initialPrompt` | string | No | Auto-submitted as the first user turn when this agent runs as the main session agent (via `--agent` or the `agent` setting). Commands and skills are processed. Prepended to any user-provided prompt |
36:| `color` | string | No | Display color for the subagent in the task list and transcript: `red`, `blue`, `green`, `yellow`, `purple`, `orange`, `pink`, or `cyan` |
